Here is the comprehensive analysis of every CLI command.

---

# Complete CLI Command Analysis

## 1. `scan` (app/commands/scan/scan.py)

**What it does:** This is the primary data-collection command. It:
1. Runs a **preflight check** (`scan_queue_preflight`) -- if a living plan has unfinished queue items, it **blocks the scan** unless `--force-rescan --attest "..."` is passed or `--profile ci` is used.
2. Prepares a `ScanRuntime` object: resolves language, loads state/config, configures runtime options, expires provisional manual-override assessments, optionally resets subjective assessments.
3. Runs **all registered detectors** for the language via `ScanOrchestrator.generate()` -> `run_scan_generation()` -> `planning.generate_findings()`. This includes augmenting with stale-exclusion and stale-wontfix findings.
4. **Merges results into persistent state** via `merge_scan_results()` -> `state_mod.merge_scan()`. This is the core state mutation: it diffs findings (new/resolved/reopened), updates dimension scores, saves state atomically.
5. **Reconciles the living plan** after merge: re-surfaces skipped items past review period, syncs unscored/stale subjective dimensions into queue, regenerates auto-clusters, syncs triage/checkpoint/create-plan workflow items, seeds/clears plan-start scores.
6. **Renders output**: diff summary, score delta, dimension deltas, noise budget visibility, plan workflow nudges, scorecard badge generation, LLM summary, auto-updates skill document.
7. **Writes query.json** with the full payload for agent consumption.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (findings, scores, stats, potentials, codebase_metrics, scan_count, last_scan, zone_distribution, scan_coverage, subjective_assessments), `.desloppify/plan.json` (reconciliation, auto-clusters, workflow items, start scores), `.desloppify/query.json`, `scorecard.png` badge image, config (clears `needs_rescan` flag)
**Dependencies:** Requires language detection, source files on disk. Gates on plan queue completion.

---

## 2. `status` (app/commands/status_cmd.py)

**What it does:** A **read-only dashboard** command that displays:
1. Score section: either "frozen" plan-start score (during active queue cycle) or live scores (overall, objective, strict, verified).
2. Queue breakdown: N items remaining (plan-ordered, skipped, subjective).
3. Scan metrics (file count, LOC).
4. Open scope breakdown (findings per path).
5. Scan completeness indicators.
6. Dimension health table (or legacy tier progress table if no dimension scores).
7. Focus suggestion (lowest dimension).
8. Subjective followup hints (when objective backlog allows).
9. Review summary, structural areas.
10. Commit tracking reminders.
11. Agent plan section from narrative engine.
12. Ignore summary.
13. Writes a status query.json payload.

Has `--json` mode that outputs a structured JSON payload.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/query.json` only
**Dependencies:** Requires completed scan.

---

## 3. `next` (app/commands/next.py)

**What it does:** Shows the **next highest-priority work item(s)** from the ranked queue. It:
1. Builds a work queue from state + plan via `build_work_queue()`.
2. Respects plan ordering, cluster focus (`active_cluster`), skipped items, subjective dimension items.
3. Supports `--count N` for multiple items, `--scope` filtering, `--group` (item/file/detector), `--cluster`, `--explain` (ranking rationale), `--include-skipped`.
4. Output formats: terminal (default), json, md. Can write to `--output FILE`.
5. Renders resolution hints for single items, commit tracking reminders, followup nudges (subjective, queue progress).
6. Writes query.json with full payload including scorecard dimensions.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/query.json` only
**Dependencies:** Requires completed scan.

---

## 4. `show` (app/commands/show/cmd.py)

**What it does:** **Drill-down view** into findings filtered by file, directory, detector, finding ID, glob, or dimension name. It:
1. Resolves the user pattern into an entity type: special view ("concerns"), subjective dimension, mechanical dimension, or file/pattern.
2. For subjective dimensions: renders assessment details (scores, components, age).
3. For mechanical dimensions: loads dimension-specific findings.
4. For files/detectors/patterns: builds a work queue filtered to scope, then renders matching findings.
5. Applies noise budget (configurable per-detector cap on displayed findings).
6. Supports `--code` (inline source snippets), `--chronic` (reopened 2+ times), `--status` filter, `--top N`, `--no-budget`, `--output FILE` (JSON), `--notes FILE`.
7. Shows agent plan section and subjective followup after findings.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/query.json` only
**Dependencies:** Requires completed scan.

---

## 5. `tree` (app/commands/viz_cmd.py -> app/output/visualize.py `cmd_tree`)

**What it does:** Renders a **text-based annotated tree** of the codebase to terminal. It:
1. Collects file data (LOC per file) using the language's file finder.
2. Builds a dependency graph using the language plugin.
3. Builds a hierarchical tree structure annotating each file/directory with LOC, fan-in/fan-out, findings count.
4. Renders the tree as indented text lines via `render_tree_lines()`.
5. Supports `--depth`, `--focus` (zoom into subdirectory), `--min-loc`, `--sort` (loc/findings/coupling), `--detail` (finding summaries per file).

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (for findings overlay), source files on disk
**State writes:** Nothing
**Dependencies:** Language detection for file finder, source files on disk.

---

## 6. `viz` (app/commands/viz_cmd.py -> app/output/visualize.py `cmd_viz`)

**What it does:** Generates an **interactive HTML treemap** visualization. It:
1. Same data collection as `tree` (file LOC, dependency graph, findings).
2. Builds a D3.js treemap JSON structure.
3. Renders into an HTML template with D3 CDN.
4. Writes to `.desloppify/treemap.html` (or `--output PATH`).
5. Prints the file URL for browser opening.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, source files on disk
**State writes:** `.desloppify/treemap.html` (or custom output path)
**Dependencies:** Language detection, source files on disk.

---

## 7. `detect` (app/commands/detect.py)

**What it does:** Runs a **single detector directly**, bypassing state tracking entirely. It:
1. Resolves the language and validates the detector name against `lang.detect_commands`.
2. Sets default thresholds for specific detectors (e.g., `large` gets `lang.large_threshold`).
3. Creates a language runtime and calls `lang.detect_commands[detector](args)` -- the raw detector function.
4. Prints results to terminal (or JSON with `--json`).
5. Does NOT update state. Prints a "Next command: `desloppify scan`" hint.

Supports detector-specific flags: `--top`, `--threshold`, `--category` (imports/vars/params/all for unused), `--file`, `--fix`, `--json`, `--lang-opt`.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (loads runtime context but does not require scan)
**State writes:** Nothing
**Dependencies:** Language detection.

---

## 8. `autofix` (app/commands/fix/cmd.py)

**What it does:** Auto-fixes mechanical issues using the language's fixer registry. It:
1. If `fixer_name == "review"`, delegates to `_cmd_fix_review()` -- the **review preparation** flow (see below).
2. Otherwise, resolves the fixer from `lang.fixers[fixer_name]`.
3. Runs the fixer's detect function to find entries.
4. Calls `fixer.fix(entries, dry_run=...)` to generate fix results.
5. In non-dry-run mode: applies changes, runs post-fix hooks (e.g., cascade import cleanup), then suggests running scan.

**The `autofix review` special case** (`fix/review_flow.py`): Prepares structured review data with dimension templates for AI evaluation. This is essentially the same thing as `review --prepare` but accessed via the autofix command. It calls `review_mod.prepare_review()` and writes to query.json with dimension prompts and file content.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, source files
**State writes:** Modifies source files (when not dry-run). Writes `.desloppify/query.json` (review mode only). Does NOT update state file.
**Dependencies:** Language detection, fixer registration.

---

## 9. `ignore` (app/commands/resolve/ignore_cmd.py)

**What it does:** Adds a pattern to the **config ignore list** and removes matching findings from state. It:
1. **Requires attestation** (anti-gaming: must include "I have actually" and "not gaming").
2. Adds pattern to `config.ignore[]` via `config_mod.add_ignore_pattern()`.
3. Sets `config.needs_rescan = true`.
4. Saves config.
5. Calls `state_mod.remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)` -- deletes matching findings from state.
6. Appends to `state.attestation_log[]`.
7. Saves state.
8. Shows score delta with plan context.
9. Writes query.json.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/config.json` (adds to `ignore[]`, sets `needs_rescan`), `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (removes findings, adds attestation log entry), `.desloppify/query.json`
**Dependencies:** None beyond state file existing.

---

## 10. `exclude` (app/commands/exclude_cmd.py)

**What it does:** Adds a path pattern to the **config exclude list** and cleans matching entries from state and plan. It:
1. Adds pattern to `config.exclude[]` via `config_mod.add_exclude_pattern()`.
2. Sets `config.needs_rescan = true`.
3. Saves config.
4. Prunes findings whose file path matches the exclusion from state (`_prune_excluded_findings`).
5. Saves state if findings were removed.
6. Purges removed finding IDs from the living plan (queue, skips, clusters).
7. Saves plan if changed.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/config.json` (adds to `exclude[]`, sets `needs_rescan`), `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (removes findings), `.desloppify/plan.json` (purges IDs)
**Dependencies:** None beyond files existing.

---

## 11. `move` (app/commands/move/move.py)

**What it does:** **Moves a file or directory** and updates all import references across the codebase. It:
1. For files: resolves source/dest paths, detects language, loads the language move module, builds dependency graph, computes import replacements, applies the move + import rewrites.
2. For directories: delegates to `run_directory_move()`.
3. Supports `--dry-run`.

**State reads:** Source files on disk, dependency graph
**State writes:** Modifies source files on disk (moves + import rewrites). Does NOT touch state/config/plan.
**Dependencies:** Language detection for move module.

---

## 12. `review` (app/commands/review/entrypoint.py)

**What it does:** A **multi-mode command** for holistic subjective code review (LLM-based). Modes:
1. **Default / `--prepare`**: Prepares structured review data (dimension prompts, file content, context) into query.json for an external reviewer.
2. **`--run-batches`**: Orchestrates automated batch review using subagent runners (Codex). Prepares packets, runs batches (potentially in parallel), merges results, imports findings, optionally runs scan after import.
3. **`--import FILE`**: Imports review findings from a JSON file into state.
4. **`--validate-import FILE`**: Validates an import payload without mutating state.
5. **`--merge`**: Merges conceptually duplicate open review findings.
6. **`--external-start`**: Starts a cloud external review session (generates blind packet + session token).
7. **`--external-submit`**: Submits external reviewer JSON via a started session.
8. **`--import-run DIR`**: Re-imports from a completed run directory.

Extensive trust/attestation options (`--manual-override`, `--attested-external`, `--attest`). Supports `--dimensions`, `--retrospective`, `--scan-after-import`.

Has a preflight (`review_rerun_preflight`) that gates re-runs when the objective plan hasn't been drained.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, source files
**State writes:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (imports subjective findings/assessments), `.desloppify/query.json`, run artifact files, potentially triggers scan
**Dependencies:** Language detection, completed scan.

---

## 13. `zone` (app/commands/zone_cmd.py)

**What it does:** Shows, sets, or clears **zone classifications** (production/test/config/generated/script/vendor) for files. It:
1. **`zone show`** (default): Lists all scanned files grouped by zone, highlighting overrides.
2. **`zone set <file> <zone>`**: Adds a zone override to config.
3. **`zone clear <file>`**: Removes a zone override from config.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (state path check), `.desloppify/config.json` (zone_overrides), source files
**State writes:** `.desloppify/config.json` (zone_overrides) for set/clear only
**Dependencies:** Language detection (for file finder and zone rules).

---

## 14. `config` (app/commands/config_cmd.py)

**What it does:** Shows, sets, or unsets **project configuration** values. It:
1. **`config show`** (default): Prints all config keys with values, defaults, and descriptions.
2. **`config set <key> <value>`**: Sets a config value (validated against schema).
3. **`config unset <key>`**: Resets a config key to its schema default.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/config.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/config.json` for set/unset
**Dependencies:** None.

---

## 15. `dev` (app/commands/dev_cmd.py)

**What it does:** Developer utilities. Currently only one subcommand:
- **`dev scaffold-lang <name>`**: Generates a standardized language plugin scaffold directory with template files (config, detectors, extractors, tests). Supports `--extension`, `--marker`, `--default-src`, `--force`. Optionally wires `pyproject.toml` testpaths.

**State reads:** `pyproject.toml`
**State writes:** Creates language plugin directory structure under `desloppify/languages/<name>/`, optionally edits `pyproject.toml`
**Dependencies:** None.

---

## 16. `langs` (app/commands/langs.py)

**What it does:** **Read-only listing** of all available language plugins. Shows a table with:
- Language name
- Integration depth (full/standard/shallow/minimal) with a visual bar
- Available tools/phases and whether auto-fix is supported

**State reads:** Language plugin registry
**State writes:** Nothing
**Dependencies:** None.

---

## 17. `update-skill` (app/commands/update_skill.py)

**What it does:** Downloads and installs/updates the desloppify skill/agent document from GitHub. It:
1. Resolves the interface (claude, codex, cursor, copilot, windsurf, gemini, opencode) from argument or existing install.
2. Downloads `SKILL.md` and optional overlay from GitHub.
3. Either replaces the section markers in a shared file or writes a dedicated file.
4. Reports the installed version.

**State reads:** Existing skill document files
**State writes:** Agent skill document (e.g., `CLAUDE.md`, `.cursorrules`, etc.)
**Dependencies:** Network access to GitHub.

---

## 18. `plan` and all subcommands (app/commands/plan/cmd.py)

### `plan` (no subcommand)
**What it does:** Generates a **full prioritized markdown plan** from state via `planning_mod.generate_plan_md(state)`. This is a rich markdown document with dimension health table, per-tier task lists, scoring model breakdown.
Also auto-resolves the `workflow::create-plan` queue item if present.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (purges create-plan workflow item if present)

### `plan show`
**What it does:** Shows **plan metadata summary**: queue size, skip counts (temp/wontfix/fp), cluster list, annotation counts, active focus, superseded count, commit tracking summary.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/plan.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`
**State writes:** Nothing

### `plan queue`
**What it does:** Renders a **compact table** of all upcoming queue items. Shows position, confidence, detector, summary, cluster. Supports `--top`, `--cluster`, `--include-skipped`, `--sort` (priority/recent). Marks new items since last triage with `*`. Collapses clusters into single rows.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** Nothing

### `plan reset`
**What it does:** Resets the plan to empty (clears queue_order, clusters, overrides, skipped, etc.). Logs the reset action.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (cleared)

### `plan move <patterns> <position>`
**What it does:** Moves findings/clusters to a position in the queue (top, bottom, before, after, up, down). Patterns can be IDs, detectors, file paths, globs, or cluster names.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (queue_order reordered)

### `plan describe <patterns> "<text>"`
**What it does:** Sets an augmented description on findings in the plan overrides.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (overrides.description)

### `plan note <patterns> "<text>"`
**What it does:** Sets a note on findings in the plan overrides.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (overrides.note)

### `plan skip <patterns>`
**What it does:** Skips findings with three modes:
- **Temporary** (default): Adds to `plan.skipped{}` with `kind: "temporary"`. Optionally `--review-after N` to re-surface after N scans. No attestation required. **Does NOT change finding status in state.**
- **Permanent** (`--permanent`): Adds to `plan.skipped{}` with `kind: "permanent"`. **Also changes finding status to "wontfix" in state** (score-affecting). Requires `--note` and `--attest`.
- **False positive** (`--false-positive`): Adds to `plan.skipped{}` with `kind: "false_positive"`. **Also changes finding status to "false_positive" in state.** Requires `--attest`.

Uses transactional persistence (rollback both files on partial failure).

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (skipped), `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (finding status -- permanent/fp only)

### `plan unskip <patterns>`
**What it does:** Brings skipped findings back to queue. For permanent/fp items, also reopens them in state (sets status back to "open"). Transactional persistence.

**State reads/writes:** Same as skip, reversed.

### `plan reopen <patterns>`
**What it does:** Reopens resolved findings from plan context. Sets finding status to "open" in state, removes from skipped in plan, adds back to queue_order, purges from commit tracking uncommitted list.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** Both files (transactional).

### `plan done <patterns>`
**What it does:** Marks findings as fixed. This is the primary "I fixed this" command. It:
1. Handles synthetic IDs (triage::, workflow::, subjective::) by purging them from plan directly.
2. For real findings: validates attestation, note length, cluster guard (blocks bulk-completing small clusters), then **delegates to `cmd_resolve`** with `status="fixed"`.
3. `cmd_resolve` changes finding status to "fixed" in state, purges from plan queue, adds to commit tracking (uncommitted), shows score delta, commit guidance, and next command hint.

**State reads:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`, `.desloppify/plan.json`
**State writes:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (finding status -> fixed), `.desloppify/plan.json` (purges IDs, adds log, adds to uncommitted), `.desloppify/query.json`

### `plan focus <cluster> | --clear`
**What it does:** Sets or clears the active cluster focus. When a cluster is focused, `next` and `plan queue` auto-scope to it.

**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (active_cluster)

### `plan cluster {create|add|remove|delete|move|show|list|update|merge}`
**What it does:** Full cluster lifecycle management:
- **create**: Creates a named cluster with optional description/action.
- **add**: Adds findings (by pattern) to cluster.
- **remove**: Removes findings from cluster.
- **delete**: Deletes cluster, orphans members.
- **move**: Moves cluster members as a block in queue order.
- **show**: Shows cluster details + member list with finding info.
- **list**: Lists all clusters with member counts.
- **update**: Updates description and/or action_steps.
- **merge**: Merges source cluster into target (moves findings, deletes source).

**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` only (clusters, queue_order)

### `plan triage`
**What it does:** Implements a **staged triage workflow**: OBSERVE -> REFLECT -> ORGANIZE -> COMMIT. This is a post-review workflow for analyzing and organizing new findings after a review run. Supports:
- `--stage observe/reflect/organize` + `--report`: Record stage reports.
- `--confirm observe/reflect/organize` + `--attestation`: Confirm completed stages.
- `--complete` + `--strategy`: Mark triage complete with strategy summary.
- `--confirm-existing` + `--confirmed` + `--note`: Fast-track confirmation of existing plan.
- `--start`: Manually inject triage items.
- `--dry-run`: Preview mode.

Enforces stage dependency chain (observe before reflect, etc.).

**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (epic_triage_meta, triage stage data)

### `plan commit-log {status|record|history|pr}`
**What it does:** Tracks commits and resolved findings for PR description updates:
- **status**: Shows git context, uncommitted/committed finding counts, PR number.
- **record**: Records HEAD commit SHA, moves uncommitted -> committed. Updates PR description via `gh` if configured.
- **history**: Shows commit records.
- **pr**: Prints PR body markdown (dry run).

**State reads:** `.desloppify/plan.json`, `.desloppify/config.json`, git context
**State writes:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (commit_log, uncommitted_findings), optionally updates GitHub PR body

---

# Focused Comparisons

## `ignore` vs `exclude` vs `plan skip`

| Aspect | `ignore` | `exclude` | `plan skip` |
|--------|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Scope** | Finding-level (pattern match on finding ID/detector/file) | Path-level (all findings in matching paths) | Finding-level (by ID/pattern) |
| **Config mutation** | Adds to `config.ignore[]` | Adds to `config.exclude[]` | No config change |
| **State mutation** | Removes matching findings from state immediately | Removes matching findings from state immediately | Temporary: NO state change. Permanent/FP: changes finding status |
| **Plan mutation** | None | Purges IDs from plan queue/clusters | Adds to `plan.skipped{}` |
| **Requires attestation** | Yes (always) | No | Only for permanent/false_positive |
| **Needs rescan** | Sets `config.needs_rescan` | Sets `config.needs_rescan` | No |
| **Persistence** | Permanent (pattern in config, findings gone) | Permanent (path excluded from future scans) | Temporary skip: auto-resurfaces. Permanent: like resolve wontfix. FP: like resolve false_positive |
| **Reversibility** | Must remove from config.ignore manually + rescan | Must remove from config.exclude manually + rescan | `plan unskip` reverses it |
| **Semantic intent** | "Never report findings matching this pattern again" | "Stop scanning these files entirely" | Temp: "I'll deal with this later". Perm: "This is intentional". FP: "Detector is wrong" |

**Key difference**: `ignore` and `exclude` are permanent configuration changes that affect future scans. `plan skip` (temporary) is a queue-management operation that hides items without changing their state -- they remain "open" in state and can reappear. `plan skip --permanent` and `plan skip --false-positive` are hybrid: they change finding status in state AND add to plan skipped.

## `show` vs `plan show` vs `plan queue`

| Aspect | `show <pattern>` | `plan show` | `plan queue` |
|--------|-----------------|-------------|--------------|
| **Purpose** | Drill into findings by file/dir/detector/ID/dimension | Plan metadata summary | Ordered table of upcoming work items |
| **What it displays** | Detailed findings: file, line, summary, code snippets, detector, confidence | Queue size, skip counts, cluster list, annotations, focus, superseded count, commit tracking | Compact numbered table: #, confidence, detector, summary, cluster |
| **Input** | Requires a pattern argument | No arguments | Optional `--cluster`, `--top`, `--sort` |
| **Data source** | Builds work queue filtered by scope | Reads plan.json metadata only | Builds full work queue with plan ordering |
| **Finding detail** | Full finding details with optional code | No finding details | One-line summary per item |
| **Overlap** | No overlap with the other two | Summary of the plan structure | Overlaps with `next --count N` in showing queue items |

`plan queue` and `next --count N` are similar but differ in that: `plan queue` collapses clusters into single rows and shows all items by default (table format), while `next` shows individual items with more context per item (terminal card format) and defaults to 1 item.

## `detect` vs `scan`

| Aspect | `detect <name>` | `scan` |
|--------|-----------------|--------|
| **Detectors run** | Single named detector | ALL registered detectors for the language |
| **State persistence** | None -- results are ephemeral | Full merge into state (new/resolved/reopened findings) |
| **Plan interaction** | None | Post-scan reconciliation (clusters, triage, workflow items, score checkpoints) |
| **Output** | Raw detector output (terminal or JSON) | Diff summary, score delta, dimension changes, narrative, badge, LLM summary |
| **Use case** | "Let me quickly check what this one detector finds" | "Run the full analysis pipeline and update everything" |
| **Flags** | Detector-specific: `--threshold`, `--category`, `--file`, `--fix` | Scan-wide: `--profile`, `--skip-slow`, `--force-resolve`, `--reset-subjective`, `--force-rescan` |
| **Queue gate** | None | Blocks if plan queue has items (unless forced) |

`detect` is a debugging/exploration tool. `scan` is the production pipeline.

## `tree` vs `viz`

| Aspect | `tree` | `viz` |
|--------|--------|-------|
| **Output format** | Plain text to terminal | HTML file for browser |
| **Interactivity** | None (static text) | D3.js treemap (zoomable, hoverable, clickable) |
| **Data shown** | File tree with LOC, findings count, coupling annotations | Same data as tree but as a color-coded area visualization |
| **Customization** | `--depth`, `--focus`, `--min-loc`, `--sort`, `--detail` | `--output` only |
| **Shared code** | Same `_collect_file_data`, `_build_dep_graph_for_path`, `_build_tree`, `_findings_by_file` | Same shared data collection |
| **Output size** | Small (terminal text) | Large (full HTML with embedded D3 + data) |

These are genuinely different output formats for the same underlying data. `tree` is for quick terminal inspection; `viz` is for deep visual exploration. They share ~80% of their implementation code. They could arguably be one command with `--format text|html` but the argument surfaces are quite different.

## `plan done` -- state transitions

`plan done <patterns>` triggers the following state transitions:

1. **Synthetic IDs** (triage::*, workflow::*, subjective::*): Purged from plan queue_order only. No state file change.
2. **Real findings**: Delegated to `cmd_resolve` with `status="fixed"`:
   - In **state file**: `finding.status` changes from `"open"` to `"fixed"`. `finding.resolved_at` is set. `finding.note` and `finding.attestation` are recorded.
   - In **plan file**: Finding IDs purged from `queue_order`. Log entry appended. Finding IDs added to `uncommitted_findings[]` (commit tracking).
   - **Score impact**: Overall/objective/strict/verified scores all recalculate based on fewer open findings. Score delta is displayed.
   - In **query.json**: Resolve payload with before/after scores, patterns, narrative.

The queue-order guard ensures you can only resolve items at the front of the queue (or use `plan move` to reorder first).

## `autofix review` -- is it a weird subcommand combo?

**Yes, it is an oddity.** `autofix review` is literally just `_cmd_fix_review()` which calls `review_mod.prepare_review()` -- the exact same thing as `review --prepare`. The only difference is the entry path:

- `desloppify autofix review` -> `fix/cmd.py` checks `fixer_name == "review"` -> `fix/review_flow.py`
- `desloppify review --prepare` -> `review/entrypoint.py` -> `review/prepare.py`

Both produce the same output (review data in query.json with dimension prompts). The `autofix review` path still shows the same "AGENT PLAN" output with review instructions. It exists as a historical artifact -- "review" was added to the autofix namespace before the dedicated `review` command existed with all its modes.

The parser lists `review` as a "special" fixer in the epilog: `"special: review -- prepare structured review data"`. It is the only "fixer" that does not actually fix anything. It is purely a data preparation step.

---

# Additional Hidden Command: `resolve` (not in CLI registry)

`cmd_resolve` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` is not exposed as a public CLI command. It is only called internally by `plan done`. It handles:
- Changing finding status (fixed/wontfix/false_positive/open) in state
- Anti-gaming validation (attestation, note length)
- Queue-order enforcement (must resolve items at front of queue)
- Triage guardrail (blocks resolving when new findings need triage)
- Cluster expansion (resolving a cluster name resolves all members)
- Plan cleanup (purge resolved IDs, commit tracking)
- Score display and next-step guidance

> DEVELOPER

I need to design a comprehensive CLI rename plan for the desloppify tool. Based on thorough analysis of every command, here are the changes I've decided on. I need you to find every file that needs to change for each rename.

## Changes

### 1. `ignore` → `suppress`
The `ignore` command adds patterns to config.ignore[] and removes matching findings from state. The word "ignore" is ambiguous (sounds like it ignores files, not findings). "Suppress" aligns with the internal model (findings get `suppressed: true` flag) and is clearly distinct from `exclude` (path exclusion) and `plan skip` (queue deferral).

Find all references to:
- Parser registration: `add_parser("ignore", ...)` and `_add_ignore_parser`
- Registry: `"ignore": cmd_ignore_pattern`  
- User-facing strings: `desloppify ignore`, help text mentioning "ignore" as a command
- Narrative/reminders: any code checking `command == "ignore"` or `command not in {"fix", "resolve", "ignore"}`
- Docs: SKILL.md, scan_reporting_text.py workflow guide
- Tests: any test referencing the `ignore` command name

### 2. `plan move` → `plan reorder`
Top-level `move` physically moves files; `plan move` reorders queue items. Same word, completely different operations. "Reorder" eliminates the collision.

Also `plan cluster move` → `plan cluster reorder` for consistency.

Find all references to:
- Parser: `plan_sub.add_parser("move", ...)` and the cluster sub `cluster_sub.add_parser("move", ...)`
- Plan command dispatcher (plan/cmd.py likely has action routing)
- User-facing strings: `desloppify plan move`, help text, epilog examples
- Docs: SKILL.md
- Tests: any test using `plan move` or `plan cluster move`

### 3. `plan done` → `plan resolve`
"Done" is ambiguous (done looking? done for now?). "Resolve" matches the internal state transition (findings go to "fixed" status), matches the hidden cmd_resolve it delegates to, and is more precise.

Find all references to:
- Parser: `plan_sub.add_parser("done", ...)`
- Plan command dispatcher
- User-facing strings: `desloppify plan done`, `plan done`, attestation examples
- Work queue helpers: resolution command suggestions (the `next` command shows "when done, run: desloppify plan done...")
- Auto cluster action suggestions
- Resolve command render (shows suggested commands)
- Docs: SKILL.md (many references), scan_reporting_text.py
- Tests

### 4. Remove `autofix review` special case
`autofix review` is redundant with `review --prepare`. A "fixer" that doesn't fix anything is confusing.

Find:
- The `if fixer_name == "review"` branch in fix/cmd.py
- fix/review_flow.py (can it be deleted?)
- Parser epilog in parser_groups_admin.py (`"special: review — prepare structured review data"`)
- Any docs/tests referencing `autofix review` or `fix review`

1→"""fix command: auto-fix mechanical issues with fixer registry and pipeline."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands._show_terminal import show_fix_dry_run_samples
     9→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import FixResult
    10→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    11→
    12→from .apply_flow import (
    13→    _apply_and_report,
    14→    _detect,
    15→    _print_fix_summary,
    16→    _report_dry_run,
    17→    _warn_uncommitted_changes,
    18→)
    19→from .options import _load_fixer
    20→from .review_flow import _cmd_fix_review
    21→
    22→
    23→def cmd_fix(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    24→    """Auto-fix mechanical issues."""
    25→    fixer_name = args.fixer
    26→    if fixer_name == "review":
    27→        _cmd_fix_review(args)
    28→        return
    29→
    30→    dry_run = getattr(args, "dry_run", False)
    31→    path = Path(args.path)
    32→
    33→    lang, fixer = _load_fixer(args, fixer_name)
    34→
    35→    if not dry_run:
    36→        _warn_uncommitted_changes()
    37→    entries = _detect(fixer, path)
    38→    if not entries:
    39→        print(colorize(f"No {fixer.label} found.", "green"))
    40→        return
    41→
    42→    raw = fixer.fix(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
    43→    if isinstance(raw, FixResult):
    44→        results = raw.entries
    45→        skip_reasons = raw.skip_reasons
    46→    else:
    47→        results = raw
    48→        skip_reasons = {}
    49→    total_items = sum(len(r["removed"]) for r in results)
    50→    total_lines = sum(r.get("lines_removed", 0) for r in results)
    51→    _print_fix_summary(fixer, results, total_items, total_lines, dry_run)
    52→
    53→    if dry_run and results:
    54→        show_fix_dry_run_samples(entries, results)
    55→
    56→    if not dry_run:
    57→        _apply_and_report(
    58→            args,
    59→            path,
    60→            fixer,
    61→            fixer_name,
    62→            entries,
    63→            results,
    64→            total_items,
    65→            lang,
    66→            skip_reasons,
    67→        )
    68→    else:
    69→        _report_dry_run(args, fixer_name, entries, results, total_items)
    70→    print()
    71→

    18→
    19→def _print_subjective_integrity_refresh_hints(state: dict) -> None:
    20→    unassessed_dims = subjective_integrity_mod.unassessed_subjective_dimensions(
    21→        state.get("dimension_scores", {})
    22→    )
    23→    scoped_findings = state_mod.path_scoped_findings(
    24→        state.get("findings", {}),
    25→        state.get("scan_path"),
    26→    )
    27→    _coverage_total, _reasons, holistic_reasons = (
    28→        subjective_integrity_mod.subjective_review_open_breakdown(scoped_findings)
    29→    )
    30→    holistic_open = sum(holistic_reasons.values())
    31→    if not (unassessed_dims or holistic_open > 0):
    32→        return
    33→
    34→    print(colorize("  Subjective integrity still needs refresh.", "yellow"))
    35→    if unassessed_dims:
    36→        rendered = ", ".join(unassessed_dims[:3])
    37→        if len(unassessed_dims) > 3:
    38→            rendered = f"{rendered}, +{len(unassessed_dims) - 3} more"
    39→        print(colorize(f"    Unassessed (0% placeholder): {rendered}", "dim"))
    40→    if holistic_open > 0:
    41→        print(
    42→            colorize(
    43→                f"    Holistic stale/missing signals: {holistic_open}", "dim"
    44→            )
    45→        )
    46→    print(
    47→        colorize(
    48→            "    Preferred: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`",
    49→            "dim",
    50→        )
    51→    )
    52→    print(
    53→        colorize(
    54→            "    Claude cloud durable path: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`, then run the printed `--external-submit ... --scan-after-import` command",
    55→            "dim",
    56→        )
    57→    )
    58→    print(
    59→        colorize(
    60→            "    Findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --prepare`, then `desloppify review --import findings.json && desloppify scan`",
    61→            "dim",
    62→        )
    63→    )
    64→
    65→
    66→def _print_dimension_prompts(data: dict) -> None:
    67→    dims = data.get("dimensions", [])
    68→    prompts = data.get("dimension_prompts", {})
    69→    for dim in dims:
    70→        prompt = prompts.get(dim)
    71→        if not prompt:
    72→            continue
    73→        print(colorize(f"  {dim}", "cyan"))
    74→        print(colorize(f"    {prompt['description']}", "dim"))
    75→        print(colorize("    Look for:", "dim"))

    81→            for item in skip:

    86→def _print_lang_guide(data: dict, *, lang_run) -> None:
    87→    lang_guide = data.get("lang_guidance") or review_mod.get_lang_guidance(
    88→        lang_run.name
    89→    )
    90→    if not lang_guide:
    91→        return
    92→    print(colorize(f"  Language: {lang_run.name}", "cyan"))
    93→    if lang_guide.get("naming"):
    94→        print(colorize(f"    Naming: {lang_guide['naming']}", "dim"))
    95→    for pattern in lang_guide.get("patterns", []):
    96→        print(colorize(f"    - {pattern}", "dim"))
    97→    print()
    98→
    99→
   100→def _print_review_agent_plan() -> None:
   101→    print(colorize("  Review data written to .desloppify/query.json", "dim"))
   102→    print(colorize("\n  AGENT PLAN (run in order):", "yellow"))
   103→    print(
   104→        colorize(
   105→            "  1. Read query.json — it includes file content, context, and prompts",
   106→            "dim",
   107→        )
   108→    )
   109→    print(colorize("  2. Evaluate each file against the dimensions above", "dim"))
   110→    print(colorize("  3. Save findings as JSON (for example: findings.json)", "dim"))
   111→    print(
   112→        colorize(
   113→            "  4. Preferred (Codex local): desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import",
   114→            "dim",
   115→        )
   116→    )
   117→    print(
   118→        colorize(
   119→            "  5. Claude cloud durable path: desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude (then run the printed --external-submit ... --scan-after-import command)",
   120→            "dim",
   121→        )
   122→    )
   123→    print(
   124→        colorize(
   125→            "  6. Findings-only fallback: desloppify review --import findings.json && desloppify scan",
   126→            "dim",
   127→        )
   128→    )
   129→    print(
   130→        colorize(
   131→            "  Next command to improve subjective scores: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`",
   132→            "dim",
   133→        )
   134→    )
   135→    print()
   136→
   137→
   138→def _cmd_fix_review(args):
   139→    """Prepare structured review data with dimension templates for AI evaluation."""
   140→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   141→    lang_cfg = resolve_lang(args)
   142→    if not lang_cfg:
   143→        raise CommandError("could not detect language. Use --lang.")
   144→
   145→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_path(args))
   146→    path = Path(args.path)
   147→
   148→    lang_run, found_files = review_runtime_mod.setup_lang_concrete(
   149→        lang_cfg,
   150→        path,
   151→        runtime.config,
   152→    )
   153→    data = review_mod.prepare_review(
   154→        path,
   155→        lang_run,
   156→        state,
   157→        options=review_mod.ReviewPrepareOptions(files=found_files or None),
   158→    )
   159→
   160→    if data["total_candidates"] == 0:
   161→        print(
   162→            colorize(
   163→                "\n  All production files have been reviewed. Nothing to do.", "green"
   164→            )
   165→        )
   166→        _print_subjective_integrity_refresh_hints(state)
   167→        return
   168→
   169→    print(
   170→        colorize(f"\n  {data['total_candidates']} files need design review\n", "bold")
   171→    )
   172→    _print_dimension_prompts(data)
   173→    _print_lang_guide(data, lang_run=lang_run)
   174→
   175→    write_query(data)
   176→    _print_review_agent_plan()
   177→

    31→def _cmd_plan_generate(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    32→    """Generate the prioritized markdown plan (existing behavior)."""

    36→        purge_ids(plan, [WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID])
    37→        save_plan(plan)

    41→def _cmd_plan_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    42→    """Show plan metadata summary."""
    43→    plan = load_plan()
    44→    ordered = len(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    45→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
    46→    total_skipped = len(skipped)
    47→    temp_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "temporary")
    48→    perm_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "permanent")
    49→    fp_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "false_positive")
    50→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    51→    overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
    52→    active = plan.get("active_cluster")
    53→    superseded = len(plan.get("superseded", {}))
    54→
    55→    # Count meaningful annotations (descriptions and notes)
    56→    described = sum(1 for o in overrides.values() if o.get("description"))
    57→    noted = sum(1 for o in overrides.values() if o.get("note"))
    58→
    59→    print(colorize("  Living Plan Status", "bold"))
    60→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 40, "dim"))
    61→    print(f"  Queue:            {ordered} items prioritized")
    62→    if total_skipped:
    63→        print(f"  Skipped:          {total_skipped} (temp: {temp_count}, wontfix: {perm_count}, fp: {fp_count})")
    64→    else:
    65→        print(f"  Skipped:          0")
    66→    print(f"  Clusters:         {len(clusters)}")
    67→    if clusters:
    68→        for name, cluster in clusters.items():
    69→            desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
    70→            member_count = len(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    71→            marker = " (focused)" if name == active else ""
    72→            desc_str = f" — {desc}" if desc else ""
    73→            print(f"    {name}: {member_count} items{desc_str}{marker}")
    74→    if described or noted:
    75→        print(f"  Annotations:      {described} described, {noted} noted")
    76→    if active:
    77→        print(f"  Focus:            {active}")
    78→    if superseded:
    79→        print(f"  Disappeared:      {superseded} (resolved or removed since last scan)")
    80→

    83→
    84→    _cfg = _load_config()

    95→            )

    98→def _cmd_plan_reset(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    99→    """Reset the plan to empty."""
   100→    plan = load_plan()
   101→    queue_len = len(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   102→    cluster_count = len(plan.get("clusters", {}))
   103→    reset_plan(plan)
   104→    append_log_entry(
   105→        plan, "reset", actor="user",
   106→        detail={"previous_queue_size": queue_len, "previous_cluster_count": cluster_count},
   107→    )
   108→    save_plan(plan)
   109→    print(colorize("  Plan reset to empty.", "green"))

   112→def cmd_plan(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   113→    """Dispatch plan subcommand or generate markdown output."""
   114→    plan_action = getattr(args, "plan_action", None)
   115→
   116→    if plan_action is None:
   117→        _cmd_plan_generate(args)
   118→        return
   119→
   120→    if plan_action == "show":
   121→        _cmd_plan_show(args)
   122→        return
   123→
   124→    if plan_action == "queue":
   125→        cmd_plan_queue(args)
   126→        return
   127→
   128→    if plan_action == "reset":
   129→        _cmd_plan_reset(args)
   130→        return
   131→
   132→    if plan_action == "move":
   133→        cmd_plan_move(args)
   134→        return
   135→
   136→    if plan_action in (
   137→        "describe", "note", "focus",
   138→        "skip", "unskip", "reopen", "done",
   139→    ):
   140→        dispatch = {
   141→            "describe": cmd_plan_describe,
   142→            "done": cmd_plan_done,
   143→            "note": cmd_plan_note,
   144→            "focus": cmd_plan_focus,
   145→            "skip": cmd_plan_skip,
   146→            "unskip": cmd_plan_unskip,
   147→            "reopen": cmd_plan_reopen,
   148→        }
   149→        dispatch[plan_action](args)
   150→        return
   151→
   152→    if plan_action == "cluster":
   153→        cmd_cluster_dispatch(args)
   154→        return
   155→
   156→    if plan_action == "triage":
   157→        from desloppify.app.commands.plan.triage_handlers import cmd_plan_triage
   158→
   159→        cmd_plan_triage(args)
   160→        return
   161→
   162→    if plan_action == "commit-log":
   163→        from desloppify.app.commands.plan.commit_log_handlers import cmd_commit_log_dispatch
   164→
   165→        cmd_commit_log_dispatch(args)
   166→        return
   167→
   168→    print(f"Unknown plan action: {plan_action}")

   170→

1→"""Plan move subcommand handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.plan._resolve import resolve_ids_from_patterns
    10→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    11→from desloppify.engine.plan import append_log_entry, load_plan, move_items, save_plan
    12→
    13→
    14→def resolve_target(plan: dict, target: str | None, position: str) -> str | None:
    15→    """Resolve a cluster name used as a before/after target to a member ID."""
    16→    if target is None:
    17→        return None
    18→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    19→    if target not in clusters:
    20→        return target
    21→    member_ids = clusters[target].get("finding_ids", [])
    22→    if not member_ids:
    23→        return target
    24→    order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    25→    member_set = set(member_ids)
    26→    ordered = [fid for fid in order if fid in member_set]
    27→    if not ordered:
    28→        return member_ids[0]
    29→    return ordered[0] if position == "before" else ordered[-1]
    30→
    31→
    32→def cmd_plan_move(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    33→    """Move findings to a position in the queue."""
    34→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    35→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    36→        return
    37→
    38→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    39→    position: str = getattr(args, "position", "top")
    40→    target: str | None = getattr(args, "target", None)
    41→
    42→    if position in ("before", "after") and target is None:
    43→        print(colorize(f"  '{position}' requires --target (-t). Example: plan move <pat> {position} -t <id>", "red"))
    44→        return
    45→    if position in ("up", "down") and target is None:
    46→        print(colorize(f"  '{position}' requires --target (-t) with an integer offset. Example: plan move <pat> {position} -t 3", "red"))
    47→        return
    48→
    49→    plan = load_plan()
    50→
    51→    target = resolve_target(plan, target, position)
    52→
    53→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    54→    if not finding_ids:
    55→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    56→        return
    57→
    58→    offset: int | None = None
    59→    if position in ("up", "down") and target is not None:
    60→        try:
    61→            offset = int(target)
    62→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
    63→            print(colorize(f"  Invalid offset: {target}", "red"))
    64→            return
    65→        target = None
    66→
    67→    count = move_items(plan, finding_ids, position, target=target, offset=offset)
    68→    append_log_entry(
    69→        plan, "move", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
    70→        detail={"position": position, "target": target, "offset": offset},
    71→    )
    72→    save_plan(plan)
    73→    print(colorize(f"  Moved {count} item(s) to {position}.", "green"))
    74→
    75→
    76→__all__ = ["cmd_plan_move", "resolve_target"]
    77→

1→"""Plan override subcommand handlers: describe, note, skip, unskip, done, reopen, focus."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan, state_path
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.plan._resolve import resolve_ids_from_patterns
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
    14→    show_attestation_requirement,
    15→    show_note_length_requirement,
    16→    validate_attestation,
    17→    validate_note_length,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    20→from desloppify.engine.plan import (
    21→    annotate_finding,
    22→    append_log_entry,
    23→    clear_focus,
    24→    describe_finding,
    25→    PLAN_FILE,
    26→    load_plan,
    27→    plan_path_for_state,
    28→    purge_ids,
    29→    save_plan,
    30→    set_focus,
    31→    skip_items,
    32→    unskip_items,
    33→)
    34→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    35→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    36→
    37→
    38→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    39→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.STATE_FILE
    40→
    41→
    42→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    43→    return path if path is not None else PLAN_FILE
    44→
    45→
    46→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    47→    if state_file is None:
    48→        return None
    49→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    50→
    51→
    52→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    53→    if not path.exists():
    54→        return None
    55→    return path.read_text()
    56→
    57→
    58→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    59→    if snapshot is None:
    60→        try:
    61→            path.unlink()
    62→        except FileNotFoundError:
    63→            return
    64→        return
    65→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    66→
    67→
    68→def _save_plan_state_transactional(
    69→    *,
    70→    plan: dict,
    71→    plan_path: Path | None,
    72→    state_data: dict,
    73→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    74→) -> None:
    75→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    76→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    77→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    78→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    79→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    80→
    81→    try:
    82→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    83→        save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    84→    except Exception:
    85→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    86→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    87→        raise
    88→
    89→
    90→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    91→    """Set augmented description on findings."""
    92→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    93→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    94→        return
    95→
    96→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    97→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
    98→
    99→    plan = load_plan()
   100→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   101→    if not finding_ids:
   102→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   103→        return
   104→
   105→    for fid in finding_ids:
   106→        describe_finding(plan, fid, text or None)
   107→    append_log_entry(
   108→        plan, "describe", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
   109→        detail={"text": text or None},
   110→    )
   111→    save_plan(plan)
   112→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
   113→
   114→
   115→def cmd_plan_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   116→    """Set note on findings."""
   117→    state = command_runtime(args).state
   118→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   119→        return
   120→
   121→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   122→    text: str | None = getattr(args, "text", None)
   123→
   124→    plan = load_plan()
   125→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   126→    if not finding_ids:
   127→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   128→        return
   129→
   130→    for fid in finding_ids:
   131→        annotate_finding(plan, fid, text)
   132→    append_log_entry(
   133→        plan, "note", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
   134→        note=text,
   135→    )
   136→    save_plan(plan)
   137→    print(colorize(f"  Set note on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
   138→
   139→
   140→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   141→# Skip / unskip
   142→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   143→
   144→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   145→    """Skip findings — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
   146→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   147→    state = runtime.state
   148→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   149→        return
   150→
   151→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   152→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
   153→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
   154→    permanent: bool = getattr(args, "permanent", False)
   155→    false_positive: bool = getattr(args, "false_positive", False)
   156→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   157→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   158→
   159→    # Determine skip kind
   160→    if false_positive:
   161→        kind = "false_positive"
   162→    elif permanent:
   163→        kind = "permanent"
   164→    else:
   165→        kind = "temporary"
   166→
   167→    # Validate requirements for permanent/false_positive
   168→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   169→        if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   170→            show_attestation_requirement(
   171→                "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
   172→                attestation,
   173→                ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
   174→            )
   175→            return
   176→        if kind == "permanent" and not note:
   177→            print(
   178→                colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
   179→                file=sys.stderr,
   180→            )
   181→            return
   182→
   183→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   184→    plan_file = _plan_file_for_state(state_file)
   185→    plan = load_plan(plan_file)
   186→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   187→    if not finding_ids:
   188→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   189→        return
   190→
   191→    # For permanent/false_positive: delegate to state layer for score impact
   192→    state_data: dict | None = None
   193→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   194→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   195→        status = "wontfix" if kind == "permanent" else "false_positive"
   196→        for fid in finding_ids:
   197→            state_mod.resolve_findings(
   198→                state_data, fid, status, note or "", attestation=attestation
   199→            )
   200→
   201→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   202→    count = skip_items(
   203→        plan,
   204→        finding_ids,
   205→        kind=kind,
   206→        reason=reason,
   207→        note=note,
   208→        attestation=attestation,
   209→        review_after=review_after,
   210→        scan_count=scan_count,
   211→    )
   212→
   213→    # Log the skip action
   214→    append_log_entry(
   215→        plan,
   216→        "skip",
   217→        finding_ids=finding_ids,
   218→        actor="user",
   219→        note=note,
   220→        detail={"kind": kind, "reason": reason},
   221→    )
   222→    if state_data is not None:
   223→        _save_plan_state_transactional(
   224→            plan=plan,
   225→            plan_path=plan_file,
   226→            state_data=state_data,
   227→            state_path_value=state_file,
   228→        )
   229→    else:
   230→        save_plan(plan, plan_file)
   231→
   232→    label = {"temporary": "Skipped", "permanent": "Wontfixed", "false_positive": "Marked false positive"}
   233→    print(colorize(f"  {label[kind]} {count} item(s).", "green"))
   234→    if review_after:
   235→        print(colorize(f"  Will re-surface after {review_after} scan(s).", "dim"))
   236→
   237→
   238→def cmd_plan_unskip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   239→    """Unskip findings — bring back to queue."""
   240→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   241→    state = runtime.state
   242→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   243→        return
   244→
   245→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   246→
   247→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   248→    plan_file = _plan_file_for_state(state_file)
   249→    plan = load_plan(plan_file)
   250→    # For unskip we need to match against all statuses (skipped items may be wontfix/fp)
   251→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan, status_filter="all")
   252→    if not finding_ids:
   253→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   254→        return
   255→
   256→    count, need_reopen = unskip_items(plan, finding_ids)
   257→    append_log_entry(
   258→        plan, "unskip", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
   259→        detail={"need_reopen": need_reopen},
   260→    )
   261→
   262→    # Reopen permanent/false_positive items in state
   263→    reopened: list[str] = []
   264→    if need_reopen:
   265→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   266→        for fid in need_reopen:
   267→            reopened.extend(state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, fid, "open"))
   268→        _save_plan_state_transactional(
   269→            plan=plan,
   270→            plan_path=plan_file,
   271→            state_data=state_data,
   272→            state_path_value=state_file,
   273→        )
   274→        print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s) in state.", "dim"))
   275→    else:
   276→        save_plan(plan, plan_file)
   277→
   278→    print(colorize(f"  Unskipped {count} item(s) — back in queue.", "green"))
   279→
   280→
   281→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   282→# Reopen
   283→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   284→
   285→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   286→    """Reopen resolved findings from plan context."""
   287→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   288→
   289→    raw_state_path = state_path(args)
   290→    state_file = raw_state_path if isinstance(raw_state_path, Path) else Path(raw_state_path) if raw_state_path else None
   291→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   292→    plan_file = _plan_file_for_state(state_file)
   293→
   294→    reopened: list[str] = []
   295→    for pattern in patterns:
   296→        reopened.extend(
   297→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "open")
   298→        )
   299→
   300→    if not reopened:
   301→        print(colorize("  No resolved findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   302→        return
   303→
   304→    # Remove from skipped if present, and ensure all reopened IDs are in queue
   305→    plan = load_plan(plan_file)
   306→
   307→    # Remove from commit tracking uncommitted list
   308→    from desloppify.engine.plan import purge_uncommitted_ids as _purge_uncommitted
   309→    _purge_uncommitted(plan, reopened)
   310→
   311→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   312→    count = 0
   313→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   314→    for fid in reopened:
   315→        if fid in skipped:
   316→            skipped.pop(fid)
   317→            count += 1
   318→        if fid not in order:
   319→            plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   320→            order.add(fid)
   321→            count += 1
   322→    append_log_entry(
   323→        plan, "reopen", finding_ids=reopened, actor="user",
   324→    )
   325→    _save_plan_state_transactional(
   326→        plan=plan,
   327→        plan_path=plan_file,
   328→        state_data=state_data,
   329→        state_path_value=state_file,
   330→    )
   331→
   332→    print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s).", "green"))
   333→    if count:
   334→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
   335→
   336→
   337→_CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD = 10
   338→
   339→
   340→def _check_cluster_guard(patterns: list[str], plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
   341→    """Return True if blocked by cluster guard, False if OK to proceed."""
   342→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   343→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   344→    for pattern in patterns:
   345→        if pattern in clusters:
   346→            cluster = clusters[pattern]
   347→            # Filter to alive findings only — stale IDs should not count
   348→            ids = [
   349→                fid for fid in cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   350→                if fid in findings and findings[fid].get("status") == "open"
   351→            ]
   352→            if len(ids) == 0:
   353→                print(colorize(
   354→                    f"\n  Cluster '{pattern}' is empty — add items before marking it done.\n",
   355→                    "yellow",
   356→                ))
   357→                print(colorize(
   358→                    f"  Use: desloppify plan cluster add {pattern} <finding-id>",
   359→                    "dim",
   360→                ))
   361→                return True  # blocked
   362→            if len(ids) <= _CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD:
   363→                _print_cluster_guard(pattern, ids, state)
   364→                return True  # blocked
   365→    return False  # OK
   366→
   367→
   368→def _print_cluster_guard(cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str], state: dict) -> None:
   369→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   370→    print(colorize(
   371→        f"\n  Cluster '{cluster_name}' has {len(finding_ids)} item(s) — mark them done individually first:\n",
   372→        "yellow",
   373→    ))
   374→    for fid in finding_ids:
   375→        f = findings.get(fid, {})
   376→        summary = f.get("summary", "(no summary)")[:80]
   377→        detector = f.get("detector", "?")
   378→        print(f"    {fid}  [{detector}]  {summary}")
   379→    print(colorize(
   380→        f"\n  Use: desloppify resolve <id> --status fixed --note '...' --attest '...'",
   381→        "dim",
   382→    ))
   383→    print(colorize(
   384→        f"  Or mark each done: desloppify plan done <id> --note '...' --confirm\n",
   385→        "dim",
   386→    ))
   387→
   388→
   389→def _is_synthetic_id(fid: str) -> bool:
   390→    """Return True if the ID is a synthetic workflow/triage item, not a real finding."""
   391→    return fid.startswith("triage::") or fid.startswith("workflow::") or fid.startswith("subjective::")
   392→
   393→
   394→def _resolve_synthetic_ids(patterns: list[str]) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
   395→    """Separate synthetic IDs from real finding patterns.
   396→
   397→    Returns (synthetic_ids, remaining_patterns).
   398→    """
   399→    synthetic = [p for p in patterns if _is_synthetic_id(p)]
   400→    remaining = [p for p in patterns if not _is_synthetic_id(p)]
   401→    return synthetic, remaining
   402→
   403→
   404→def _blocked_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
   405→    """Return ``{stage_id: [blocked_by_ids]}`` for triage stages that can't run yet.
   406→
   407→    Uses the dependency graph and confirmed-stage metadata directly —
   408→    no state needed, no queue item construction.
   409→    """
   410→    from desloppify.app.commands.plan.triage_playbook import TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES
   411→    from desloppify.engine.plan import TRIAGE_IDS, TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS
   412→
   413→    order_set = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   414→    present = order_set & TRIAGE_IDS
   415→    if not present:
   416→        return {}
   417→
   418→    confirmed = set(plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {}).get("triage_stages", {}).keys())
   419→    stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "commit")
   420→
   421→    blocked: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   422→    for sid, name in zip(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS, stage_names):
   423→        if sid not in present or name in confirmed:
   424→            continue
   425→        deps = TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES.get(name, set())
   426→        unmet = sorted(
   427→            f"triage::{dep}" for dep in deps
   428→            if f"triage::{dep}" in present and dep not in confirmed
   429→        )
   430→        if unmet:
   431→            blocked[sid] = unmet
   432→    return blocked
   433→
   434→
   435→def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   436→    """Mark findings as fixed — delegates to cmd_resolve for rich UX."""
   437→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   438→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   439→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   440→
   441→    # --confirm: auto-generate attestation from --note
   442→    if getattr(args, "confirm", False):
   443→        if not note:
   444→            print(colorize("  --confirm requires --note to describe what you did.", "red"))
   445→            return
   446→        attestation = f"I have actually {note} and I am not gaming the score."
   447→        args.attest = attestation
   448→
   449→    # Handle synthetic IDs (triage::*, workflow::*, subjective::*) directly
   450→    synthetic_ids, real_patterns = _resolve_synthetic_ids(patterns)
   451→    if synthetic_ids:
   452→        plan = load_plan()
   453→        # Validate triage dependency chain
   454→        blocked_map = _blocked_triage_stages(plan)
   455→        for sid in synthetic_ids:
   456→            if sid in blocked_map:
   457→                deps = ", ".join(b.replace("triage::", "") for b in blocked_map[sid])
   458→                print(colorize(f"  Cannot resolve {sid} — blocked by: {deps}", "red"))
   459→                print(colorize("  Complete those stages first, or use --force-resolve to override.", "dim"))
   460→                if not getattr(args, "force_resolve", False):
   461→                    return
   462→        purge_ids(plan, synthetic_ids)
   463→        append_log_entry(
   464→            plan, "done", finding_ids=synthetic_ids, actor="user", note=note,
   465→        )
   466→        save_plan(plan)
   467→        for sid in synthetic_ids:
   468→            print(colorize(f"  Resolved: {sid}", "green"))
   469→        if not real_patterns:
   470→            return
   471→        # Continue with remaining real patterns
   472→        patterns = real_patterns
   473→        args.patterns = patterns
   474→
   475→    # Validate note length
   476→    if not validate_note_length(note):
   477→        show_note_length_requirement(note)
   478→        return
   479→
   480→    # Pre-validate attestation before delegating (avoids stale hint in resolve)
   481→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   482→        show_attestation_requirement("Plan done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   483→        return
   484→
   485→    # Cluster completion guard: block bulk-completing small clusters
   486→    try:
   487→        runtime = command_runtime(args)
   488→        state = runtime.state
   489→        plan = load_plan()
   490→        if _check_cluster_guard(patterns, plan, state):
   491→            return
   492→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   493→        plan = None
   494→
   495→    # Log the done action (best-effort)
   496→    try:
   497→        if plan is None:
   498→            plan = load_plan()
   499→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   500→        cluster_name = None
   501→        for p in patterns:
   502→            if p in clusters:
   503→                cluster_name = p
   504→                break
   505→        append_log_entry(
   506→            plan,
   507→            "done",
   508→            finding_ids=patterns,
   509→            cluster_name=cluster_name,
   510→            actor="user",
   511→            note=note,
   512→        )
   513→        save_plan(plan)
   514→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as exc:
   515→        print(colorize(f"  Note: unable to append plan done log entry ({exc}).", "dim"))
   516→
   517→    # Deferred import: cmd_resolve has a heavy import chain that isn't needed
   518→    # for synthetic-ID handling above.
   519→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_resolve
   520→
   521→    # Build a Namespace that cmd_resolve expects
   522→    resolve_args = argparse.Namespace(
   523→        status="fixed",
   524→        patterns=patterns,
   525→        note=note,
   526→        attest=attestation,
   527→        confirm_batch_wontfix=False,
   528→        force_resolve=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
   529→        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
   530→        lang=getattr(args, "lang", None),
   531→        path=getattr(args, "path", None),
   532→        exclude=getattr(args, "exclude", None),
   533→    )
   534→
   535→    cmd_resolve(resolve_args)
   536→
   537→
   538→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   539→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   540→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   541→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   542→
   543→    plan = load_plan()
   544→    if clear_flag:
   545→        prev = plan.get("active_cluster")
   546→        clear_focus(plan)
   547→        append_log_entry(
   548→            plan, "focus", actor="user",
   549→            detail={"action": "clear", "previous": prev},
   550→        )
   551→        save_plan(plan)
   552→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   553→        return
   554→
   555→    if not cluster_name:
   556→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   557→        if active:
   558→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   559→        else:
   560→            print("  No active focus.")
   561→        return
   562→
   563→    try:
   564→        set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   565→    except ValueError as ex:
   566→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   567→        return
   568→    append_log_entry(
   569→        plan, "focus", cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user",
   570→        detail={"action": "set"},
   571→    )
   572→    save_plan(plan)
   573→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   574→
   575→
   576→__all__ = [
   577→    "cmd_plan_describe",
   578→    "cmd_plan_done",
   579→    "cmd_plan_focus",
   580→    "cmd_plan_note",
   581→    "cmd_plan_reopen",
   582→    "cmd_plan_skip",
   583→    "cmd_plan_unskip",
   584→]

   143→def _cmd_cluster_move(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   144→    raw_names: str = getattr(args, "cluster_names", "") or getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
   145→    cluster_names: list[str] = [n.strip() for n in raw_names.split(",") if n.strip()]
   146→    position: str = getattr(args, "position", "top")
   147→    target: str | None = getattr(args, "target", None)
   148→
   149→    plan = load_plan()
   150→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   151→
   152→    # Validate all names exist
   153→    for name in cluster_names:
   154→        if name not in clusters:
   155→            print(colorize(f"  Cluster {name!r} does not exist.", "red"))
   156→            return
   157→
   158→    # Resolve cluster-name targets for before/after

   161→    offset: int | None = None

   163→        try:
   164→            offset = int(target)
   165→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   166→            print(colorize(f"  Invalid offset: {target}", "red"))
   167→            return
   168→        target = None
   169→
   170→    # Collect all member IDs from all clusters, preserving order, deduplicating
   171→    seen: set[str] = set()
   172→    all_member_ids: list[str] = []
   173→    for name in cluster_names:
   174→        for fid in clusters[name].get("finding_ids", []):
   175→            if fid not in seen:
   176→                seen.add(fid)
   177→                all_member_ids.append(fid)
   178→
   179→    if not all_member_ids:
   180→        print(colorize("  No members in the specified cluster(s).", "yellow"))
   181→        return
   182→
   183→    count = move_items(plan, all_member_ids, position, target=target, offset=offset)
   184→    append_log_entry(
   185→        plan, "cluster_move", cluster_name=",".join(cluster_names), actor="user",
   186→        detail={"position": position, "count": count},
   187→    )
   188→    save_plan(plan)
   189→    label = ", ".join(cluster_names)
   190→    print(colorize(f"  Moved cluster(s) {label} ({count} items) to {position}.", "green"))

     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→

     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang

    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import show_score_with_plan_context
    12→from desloppify.core import config as config_mod
    13→from desloppify.core.exception_sets import CommandError
    14→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    15→from desloppify.core.tooling import check_config_staleness
    16→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    17→from desloppify.intelligence import narrative as narrative_mod
    18→
    19→from .persist import _save_config_or_exit, _save_state_or_exit
    20→from .selection import show_attestation_requirement, validate_attestation
    21→
    22→
    23→def cmd_ignore_pattern(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    24→    """Add a pattern to the ignore list."""
    25→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    26→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
    27→        show_attestation_requirement("Ignore", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
    28→        raise CommandError("Ignore requires a valid attestation.")
    29→
    30→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    31→    state_file = runtime.state_path
    32→    state = runtime.state
    33→    prev = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    34→
    35→    config = runtime.config
    36→    config_mod.add_ignore_pattern(config, args.pattern)
    37→    config["needs_rescan"] = True
    38→    _save_config_or_exit(config)
    39→
    40→    removed = state_mod.remove_ignored_findings(state, args.pattern)
    41→    state.setdefault("attestation_log", []).append(
    42→        {
    43→            "timestamp": state.get("last_scan"),
    44→            "command": "ignore",
    45→            "pattern": args.pattern,
    46→            "attestation": attestation,
    47→            "affected": removed,
    48→        }
    49→    )
    50→    _save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
    51→
    52→    print(colorize(f"Added ignore pattern: {args.pattern}", "green"))
    53→    if removed:
    54→        print(f"  Removed {removed} matching findings from state.")
    55→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
    56→    if config_warning:
    57→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
    58→    show_score_with_plan_context(state, prev)
    59→
    60→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    61→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
    62→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
    63→        state,
    64→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="ignore"),
    65→    )
    66→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    67→    write_query(
    68→        {
    69→            "command": "ignore",
    70→            "pattern": args.pattern,
    71→            "removed": removed,
    72→            "overall_score": scores.overall,
    73→            "objective_score": scores.objective,
    74→            "strict_score": scores.strict,
    75→            "verified_strict_score": scores.verified,
    76→            "attestation": attestation,
    77→            "narrative": narrative,
    78→        }
    79→    )
    80→
    81→
    82→__all__ = ["cmd_ignore_pattern"]
    83→
